Multimodal: Text, vision, and function calling\r\n  - Cross-Platform: .NET and Python implementations\r\n\r\n### Installation\r\nPlease visit this link [package_installation.md](https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework/blob/main/user-documentation-python/getting-started/package_installation.md) for how to instal the agent-framework package from the GitHub repo in a virtual environment.\r\n\r\nFollow this steps:\r\n- Create a requirements.txt and constraints.txt\r\n- Create and activate a virtual environment\r\n- Install the main package with extras from the repo using pip or uv\r\n- Verify the installation\r\n\r\nQuick summary:\r\n- Minimum Python: >= 3.10 (project requires-python = \">=3.10\").\r\n- GitHub repository: https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework.\r\n- Package subdirectories used by pip:\r\n\t- Main package: `python/packages/main`\r\n\t- Azure: `python/packages/azure`\r\n\t- Foundry: `python/packages/foundry`\r\n\t- Workflow: `python/packages/workflow`\r\n\r\nFor example, when you want to use Agent Framework with Azure OpenAI clients, use a requirements.txt like this:\r\n```\r\nagent-framework[azure] @ git+https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework.git@main#subdirectory=python/packages/main\r\n```\r\n\r\nwith constraints for the extras, in constraints.txt:\r\n\r\n```\r\nagent-framework-azure @ git+https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework.git@main#subdirectory=python/packages/azure\r\nagent-framework-foundry @ git+https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework.git@main#subdirectory=python/packages/foundry\r\nagent-framework-workflow @ git+https://github.com/microsoft/agent-framework.git@main#subdirectory=python/packages/workflow\r\n```\r\n\r\nThen install with:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\npip install -r requirements.txt --constraint constraints.txt\r\n```\r\n\r\n## 3. How can I assist you today?\r\n```\r\n\r\n#### Add tool\r\nTools (or Function Callings) can let Agent interact with external APIs or services, enhancing its capabilities.\r\n\r\n``` python\r\nfrom random import randint\r\nfrom typing import Annotated\r\n\r\n# Define tool(s) and add to 'ChatAgent'\r\ndef get_weather(\r\n    location: Annotated[str, \"The location to get the weather for.\"],\r\n) -> str:\r\n    \"\"\"Get the weather for a given location.\"\"\"\r\n    conditions = [\"sunny\", \"cloudy\", \"rainy\", \"stormy\"]\r\n    return f\"The weather in {location} is {conditions[randint(0, 3)]} with a high of {randint(10, 30)}°C.\"\r\n\r\nasync def quick_start_tools() -> None:\r\n    #...\r\n    agent=ChatAgent(\r\n        chat_client=chatClient,\r\n        name=\"MyAgent\",\r\n        instructions=\"You are a helpful agent.\",\r\n        tools=[get_weather],\r\n    )\r\n\r\n    result=await agent.run(\"What's the weather like in Seattle?\");\r\n    print(f\"Agent: {result.text}\") # The weather in Seattle is rainy with a high of 18°C.\r\n```\r\n\r\n#### Multi-turn Conversation with Thread\r\nThread persistence across multiple conversations.\r\n\r\n``` python\r\nasync def quick_start_thread() -> None:\r\n    #...\r\n    agent=ChatAgent(\r\n        # settings...\r\n    )\r\n    # Create a new thread that will be reused\r\n    thread = agent.get_new_thread()\r\n\r\n    # First conversation\r\n    result1 = await agent.run(\"What's the weather like in Seattle?\", thread=thread)\r\n    print(f\"Agent: {result1.text}\") # The weather in Seattle is rainy with a high of 18°C.\r\n\r\n    # Second conversation using the same thread - maintains context\r\n    result2 = await agent.run(\"Pardon?\", thread=thread)\r\n    print(f\"Agent: {result2.text}\") # Sure. How can I assist you today?\r\n```\r\n\r\n#### Add tool\r\nTools (or Function Callings) can let Agent interact with external APIs or services, enhancing its capabilities.\r\n\r\n``` python\r\nfrom random import randint\r\nfrom typing import Annotated\r\n\r\n# Define tool(s) and add to 'ChatAgent'\r\ndef get_weather(\r\n    location: Annotated[str, \"The location to get the weather for.\"],\r\n) -> str:\r\n    \"\"\"Get the weather for a given location.\"\"\"\r\n    conditions = [\"sunny\", \"cloudy\", \"rainy\", \"stormy\"]\r\n    return f\"The weather in {location} is {conditions[randint(0, 3)]} with a high of {randint(10, 30)}°C.\"\r\n\r\nasync def quick_start_tools() -> None:\r\n    #...\r\n    agent=ChatAgent(\r\n        chat_client=chatClient,\r\n        name=\"MyAgent\",\r\n        instructions=\"You are a helpful agent.\",\r\n        tools=[get_weather],\r\n    )\r\n\r\n    result=await agent.run(\"What's the weather like in Seattle?\");\r\n    print(f\"Agent: {result.text}\") # The weather in Seattle is rainy with a high of 18°C.\r\n```\r\n\r\n#### Multi-turn Conversation with Thread\r\nThread persistence across multiple conversations.\r\n\r\n``` python\r\nasync def quick_start_thread() -> None:\r\n    #...\r\n    agent=ChatAgent(\r\n        # settings...\r\n    )\r\n    # Create a new thread that will be reused\r\n    thread = agent.get_new_thread()\r\n\r\n    # First conversation\r\n    result1 = await agent.run(\"What's the weather like in Seattle?\", thread=thread)\r\n    print(f\"Agent: {result1.text}\") # The weather in Seattle is rainy with a high of 18°C.\r\n\r\n    # Second conversation using the same thread - maintains context\r\n    result2 = await agent.run(\"Pardon?\", thread=thread)\r\n    print(f\"Agent: {result2.text}\") # Sure. Install Dependencies\\n\\nFirst, create a virtual environment and install the required packages:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Create virtual environment\\npython -m venv weather-agent-env\\n\\n# Activate virtual environment\\n# On Windows:\\nweather-agent-env\\\\Scripts\\\\activate\\n# On macOS/Linux:\\nsource weather-agent-env/bin/activate\\n\\n# Install dependencies\\npip install -r requirements.txt --constraint constraints.txt\\n```\\n\\n### 2. Environment Configuration\\n\\nCopy the `.env` file and update it with your credentials:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Copy the example environment file\\ncp .env .env.local\\n```\\n\\nUpdate the `.env` file with your credentials:\\n\\n```env\\n# Required: GitHub Personal Access Token for accessing GitHub models\\nGITHUB_TOKEN=your_github_token_here\\n\\n# Optional: OpenWeatherMap API Key for real weather data\\nWEATHER_API_KEY=your_openweathermap_api_key_here\\n\\n# Model configuration\\nMODEL_ID=openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\n```\\n\\n#### Getting a GitHub Token\\n\\n1. Select the appropriate scopes (for GitHub models, basic access is sufficient)\\n5. Copy the generated token to your `.env` file\\n\\n#### Getting an OpenWeatherMap API Key (Optional)\\n\\n1. Sign up at [OpenWeatherMap](https://openweathermap.org/api)\\n2. Get your free API key\\n3. Add it to your `.env` file\\n\\nIf you don't provide a weather API key, the agent will use simulated weather data.\\n\\n## Usage\\n\\n### Basic Usage\\n\\nRun the weather agent:\\n\\n```bash\\npython weather_agent.py\\n```\\n\\nThe agent will start in interactive mode where you can ask weather questions like:\\n- \\\"What's the weather like in London?\\\"\\n- \\\"How's the weather in Tokyo today?\\\"\\n- \\\"Can you give me a 5-day forecast for New York?\\\"\\n\\n### Using in Your Code\\n\\n```python\\nimport asyncio\\nfrom weather_agent import WeatherAgent\\n\\nasync def example():\\n    # Environment variables (copy and configure)\\n└── README.md            # This file\\n```\\n\\n## How It Works\\n\\n1. **Agent Framework**: Uses Microsoft Agent Framework to create an AI agent\\n2. **GitHub Models**: Leverages GitHub's hosted AI models for natural language understanding\\n3. **Function Calling**: The agent can call weather functions to retrieve real data\\n4. **Context Management**: Maintains conversation context across multiple interactions\\n\\n## Available Models\\n\\nThe agent uses GitHub models by default. You can change the model by updating the `MODEL_ID` in your `.env` file. Some available options:\\n\\n- `openai/gpt-4.1-mini` (default) - Fast and cost-effective\\n- `openai/gpt-4.1` - More capable for complex queries\\n- `openai/gpt-4o-mini` - Multimodal capabilities\\n- `microsoft/phi-4-mini-instruct` - Smaller, efficient model\\n\\n## Troubleshooting\\n\\n### Common Issues\\n\\n1. **Missing GitHub Token**: Make sure you've set `GITHUB_TOKEN` in your `.env` file\\n2. **Installation Issues**: Ensure you're using Python 3.10 or later\\n3. **Network Issues**: The agent requires internet access to reach GitHub models and weather APIs\\n\\n### Error Messages\\n\\n- `GITHUB_TOKEN environment variable is required`: Add your GitHub token to `.env`\\n- `Error fetching weather data`: Check your `WEATHER_API_KEY` or use simulated mode\\n- `Model not found`: Verify your `MODEL_ID` is correct and available\\n\\n## Extending the Agent\\n\\nYou can easily extend the agent with additional tools:\\n\\n```python\\n# Add to weather_tools.py\\ndef get_air_quality(location: str) -> str:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Get air quality information for a location.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Implementation here\\n    pass\\n\\n# Add to agent tools in weather_agent.py\\nself.agent = ChatAgent(\\n    # ... other parameters\\n    tools=[weather_function, get_weather_forecast, get_air_quality],\\n)\\n```\\n\\n## License\\n\\nThis project is open source and available under the MIT License.\", \"filePath\": \"/C:/Users/dol/workspace/code/vscode-copilot-chat-sbh/test/scenarios/test-agent-code-gen/results/workspace1/README.md\"}"},"type":"function"}]},{"role":"tool","content":"The following files were successfully edited:\n/C:/Users/dol/workspace/code/vscode-copilot-chat-sbh/test/scenarios/test-agent-code-gen/results/workspace1/README.md","tool_call_id":"toolu_vrtx_01R9BiznkYkCjFWqFowLTMPQ","copilot_cache_control":{"type":"ephemeral"}}]
